\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\big(e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}e+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{1}{6}\,\Big(e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)+e\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{l}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt+\mathsf{R}_{k}\mathsf{R}_{l}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{m}\mathsf{R}^{l}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{m}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\Big)\ .

(6.25)

According to (
5.30
), the field equations transform under braided gauge transformations as in (
6.12
), with

\displaystyle\delta_{(\xi,\rho)}^{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}F^{\star}_{e}=-\rho\triangleright_{\star}F_{e}^{\star}+\mathrm{L}_{\xi}^{\star}F_{e}^{\star}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\delta_{(\xi,\rho)}^{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}F^{\star}_{\omega}=-\rho\triangleright_{\star}F_{\omega}^{\star}+\mathrm{L}_{\xi}^{\star}F_{\omega}^{\star}\ .

(6.26)

The goal is now to simplify these field equations using various
{\mathcal{R}}
-matrix identities to make the terms appear in manifestly covariant forms, as would be naively expected from the covariance property that is guaranteed by the general theory of Section
5.6
. This will introduce the curvature
2
-form
R^{\star}
defined analogously to (
6.6
), as well as the left and right torsion
2
-forms
T_{\textrm{\tiny L}}^{\star}
and
T_{\textrm{\tiny R}}^{\star}
from (
6.7
) and (
6.8
).

Braided torsion condition

Let us start with the second component
F_{\omega}^{\star}
. We will show that

\displaystyle F^{\star}_{\omega}=\tfrac{1}{6}\,\big(e\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny L}}^{\star}-T_{{\textrm{\tiny R}}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e-\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)-\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny R}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\big)

This expression is manifestly braided covariant, and it reduces exactly (with the correct numerical factor) to the expected torsion form
F_{\omega}=e\curlywedge T
in the classical case.

For this, we use the Yang–Baxter equation (
3.4
) for the
{\mathcal{R}}
-matrix to write the last term in (
6.3
) as

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{m}\mathsf{R}_{l}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}^{m}\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)=\mathsf{R}_{m}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{m}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\ .

Using then braided antisymmetry of the
\curlywedge_{\star}
-product on the latter, second and seventh terms, we get

\displaystyle F^{\star}_{\omega}=\frac{1}{6}\,\Big(

\displaystyle 3\,e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}e-3\,\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star}e+e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)+e\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{l}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)-(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)\curlywedge_{\star}e-\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\curlywedge_{\star}e\Big)\ .

(6.30)

Now notice that

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}(\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}e)=-\omega\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)+(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)\curlywedge_{\star}e

(6.31)

and

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{l}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega\big)

\displaystyle=-\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega-\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\mathsf{R}_{l}(\omega)\big)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)

\displaystyle=-(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega-\mathsf{R}_{k}(e\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)

\displaystyle=-(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega+e\curlywedge_{\star}(e\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega)\ .

(6.32)

Substituting both (
6.31
) and (
6.3
) in (
6.3
), and recalling that

\displaystyle e\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega:=-\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\triangleright_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)=-\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\ ,

after cancelling terms we find

\displaystyle F^{\star}_{\omega}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\,\Big(3\,e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}e-3\,\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star}e+e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)-\omega\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)-(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega

\displaystyle\quad\,\hskip 28.45274pt-\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\curlywedge_{\star}e\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\,\Big(e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}e+e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)-\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star}e-\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\curlywedge_{\star}e-\mathrm{d}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)-\omega\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)

\displaystyle\quad\,\hskip 28.45274pt-\mathrm{d}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)-(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega\Big)\ .

After identifying left and right braided covariant derivatives, we finally arrive at (
6.3
).

Braided Einstein equation

Next consider
F^{\star}_{e}
, which is our braided noncommutative analog of the four-dimensional Einstein equation with cosmological constant (that is, before solving for the Levi–Civita connection using the classical torsion equation). We will show that

\displaystyle F^{\star}_{e}=\tfrac{1}{6}\,\big(2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}+2\,R^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e+6\,\Lambda\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e

\quad\,\hskip 56.9055pt+\,e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\big)

The first three terms are manifestly braided covariant, but the rest are not. However, a few lines of calculations shows that the last three terms form a braided covariant combination, as expected from the general covariance property which is guaranteed by the braided homotopy calculations of Section
5.6
. In the classical case, this expression reduces exactly (with the correct numerical factors) to the expected curvature form
F_{e}=e\curlywedge R+\Lambda\,e\curlywedge e\curlywedge e
.

Starting from (
6.3
), we use braided antisymmetry on the second and last terms to write

\displaystyle F^{\star}_{e}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big(e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+\tfrac{1}{3}\,e\curlywedge_{\star}[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}+\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e+\tfrac{1}{3}\,[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e+2\,\Lambda\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e

\displaystyle\quad\,\hskip 28.45274pt+\tfrac{1}{3}\,\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big(e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}+R^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e+2\,\Lambda\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e

\displaystyle\quad\,\hskip 28.45274pt-\tfrac{1}{6}\,e\curlywedge_{\star}[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e+\tfrac{1}{3}\,\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\big)\ ,

which is easily brought to the form (
6.3
). It is somewhat surprising and remarkable that the second line of (
6.3
) is braided covariant, despite not being expressible in terms of manifestly covariant objects. It is instructive to explicitly demonstrate how this happens. For this, consider a left braided
\mathfrak{so}(4)
gauge transformation of the first term there:

\displaystyle\delta_{(0,\rho)}^{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega)

\displaystyle=-(\rho\star e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\big(\mathsf{R}^{k}(\mathrm{d}\rho)-[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\rho),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle=-(\rho\star e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega-\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\mathrm{d}\rho),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}-\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\rho),\mathrm{d}\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}

\displaystyle=-\rho\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega)-\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\mathrm{d}\rho),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\ ,

(6.37)

where in the first and last equalities we used the left braided Leibniz rule. Similarly, one calculates

\displaystyle\delta_{(0,\rho)}^{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}(\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e)=-\rho\triangleright_{\star}(\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e)-[\mathrm{d}\rho,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e\ .

(6.38)

The non-covariant terms involving
\mathrm{d}\rho
in (
6.3
) and (
6.38
) cancel exactly with the corresponding non-covariant terms in the gauge transformation of the last term in (
6.3
): after a couple of lines of similar manipulations we find

\displaystyle\delta_{(0,\rho)}^{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle=-\rho\triangleright_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\big)+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\mathrm{d}\rho),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}

\displaystyle\quad\,+\mathsf{R}_{k}\mathsf{R}_{l}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}\mathsf{R}_{m}(\omega),\mathsf{R}^{l}\mathsf{R}^{m}(\mathrm{d}\rho)]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\ .

(6.39)

The second term in (
6.3
) cancels the non-covariant term in (
6.3
). Using the Yang–Baxter equation (
3.4
), the last term in (
6.3
) can be written as

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{l}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}_{m}\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\mathsf{R}^{m}\mathsf{R}^{l}(\mathrm{d}\rho)]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{R}_{l}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{l}(\mathrm{d}\rho),\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega)]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}([\mathrm{d}\rho,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star})

\displaystyle=[\mathrm{d}\rho,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e\ ,

which cancels the remaining non-covariant term in (
6.38
); here we use braided symmetry of the braided Lie bracket, then the second
{\mathcal{R}}
-matrix identity from (
3.2
) followed by braided symmetry of the
\curlywedge_{\star}
-product. Altogether, we have explicitly demonstrated (
6.26
), as expected on general grounds from the proof using the braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure in Section
5.6
.

The field equations (
6.3
) and (
6.3
) of braided noncommutative gravity in four dimensions together are one of the main results of this paper. They are drastically different from the field equations previously suggested for the first order formulation of noncommutative gravity. Besides the universal (dimension-independent) differences that we discussed in the three-dimensional case of Section
6.2
, where the braided field equations were relatively mild “naive” deformations of the classical equations, here we see that the explicit forms of the braided field equations are very different and not simply “naive” deformations: the properties of the field equations of braided gravity vary drastically with dimension, in contrast to the classical case. In particular, the torsion equation
F_{\omega}^{\star}=0
now involves more braided covariant forms than just those constructed from the left and right torsion forms. The curvature equation
F_{e}^{\star}=0
is even more striking, as in addition to the anticipated braided covariant forms, there is a further non-manifestly covariant combination whose individual forms are not braided covariant on their own. These latter forms could not have been guessed or even motivated solely by deformation of the classical equations. They are a necessary and natural consequence of twisting the kinematical and dynamical content simultaneously via the braided
L_{\infty}
-algebra approach to noncommutative field theory: in this approach braided gauge covariance is formulated for the doublet of fields
A=(e,\omega)\in V_{1}
(and the corresponding doublet of field equations), which allows for non-trivial mixing of the component fields
e
and
\omega
in non-manifestly covariant ways, but in such a way that the braided homotopy relations ensure that all combinations are covariant under braided gauge transformations.

Generally, we expect this complexity to increase as the number and orders of non-zero brackets
\ell_{n}^{\star}
for
n>2
increases. In this sense braided noncommutative field theories generally contain many more covariant forms than naively expected from classical considerations.

Braided Noether identities

By (
5.7
), the gauge redundancies of braided gravity in four dimensions are encoded by the braided Noether identities

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{(e,\omega)}^{\star}F^{\star}_{(e,\omega)}

\displaystyle=\ell_{1}^{\star}\big(F_{e}^{\star},F_{\omega}^{\star}\big)-\frac{1}{12}\,\ell_{1}^{\star}\big(\ell_{3}^{\star}((e,\omega),(e,\omega),(e,\omega))\big)+\frac{1}{4}\,\ell_{2}^{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(e,\omega)\,,\,\ell_{2}^{\star}(\mathsf{R}^{k}(e,\omega),(e,\omega))\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{1}{12}\,\Big(\ell_{2}^{\star}\big(\ell_{3}^{\star}((e,\omega),(e,\omega),(e,\omega))\,,\,(e,\omega)\big)-\ell_{2}^{\star}\big((e,\omega)\,,\,\ell_{3}^{\star}((e,\omega),(e,\omega),(e,\omega))\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\,-\frac{1}{2}\,\Big(\ell_{2}^{\star}\big((e,\omega)\,,\,(F_{e}^{\star},F_{\omega}^{\star})\big)-\ell_{2}^{\star}\big((F_{e}^{\star},F_{\omega}^{\star})\,,\,(e,\omega)\big)\Big)\ =\ (0,0)\ ,

(6.40)

where we again used the slightly simpler form (
5.7
), along with
\ell_{1}^{\star}(e,\omega)=(0,0)
. As previously, we rewrite these as differential identities among the field equations (
6.3
) and (
6.3
).

The braided Noether identity for local
\mathfrak{so}(4)
rotations, in
\Omega^{4}\big(M,\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}\mathbbm{R}^{4}\big)[[\hbar]]
, is given by the second entry of (
6.3
). Inserting the corresponding brackets from (
6.21
)–(
6.3
), using the usual
{\mathcal{R}}
-matrix identities from (
3.4
) and (
3.2
), and collecting terms using (
6.31
) and (
6.3
), we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,\Big(\mathrm{d}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}^{\omega}F_{\omega}^{\star}+\mathrm{d}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny R}}}^{\omega}F_{\omega}^{\star}+\frac{3}{2}\,\mathsf{R}_{k}(F_{e}^{\star})\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)+\frac{3}{2}\,F_{e}^{\star}\wedge_{\star}e\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{4}\,\Big(\frac{1}{3}\,\big(\mathrm{d}\Phi^{\star}_{(e,\omega)}-[\Phi^{\star}_{(e,\omega)},\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}-[\omega,\Phi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star}]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\big)-\frac{1}{2}\,\big(\widetilde{\Phi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)}\wedge_{\star}e+\mathsf{R}_{l}(\widetilde{\Phi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)})\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}(e)\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{3}{2}\,\big(e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{k}(\mathrm{d}\omega)+\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\big)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)+[\mathrm{d}(e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)),\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega)]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-3\,\Lambda\,(e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\wedge_{\star}e-3\,\Lambda\,\mathsf{R}_{k}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\Big)\ =\ 0\ ,

(6.41)

where we defined

\displaystyle\Phi^{\star}_{(e,\omega)}

\displaystyle=e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)-(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e))\curlywedge_{\star}e-\omega\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)-(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega\ ,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)}

\displaystyle=e\curlywedge_{\star}[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}+\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}+[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e\ .

In the classical case, only the first line of (
6.3
) survives and reduces to the expected Noether identity
\mathrm{d}^{\omega}F_{\omega}=-\frac{3}{2}\,F_{e}\wedge e
, which follows from (but is no longer equivalent to) the first Bianchi identity
\mathrm{d}^{\omega}T=R\wedge e

[
30
]
.

The Noether identity corresponding to the braided diffeomorphism invariance of the four-dimensional theory is valued in
\Omega^{1}(M)\otimes\Omega^{4}(M)[[\hbar]]
and is given by the first entry of (
6.3
). The contributions from the first two terms vanish by definition of the differential
\ell_{1}^{\star}
, while the last line contributes terms of the same form as in the three-dimensional case, up to signs. The remaining terms can be expanded by commuting the exterior derivative
\mathrm{d}
with the legs of the twist, along with the identities (
6.31
) and (
6.3
). Altogether, the explicit form of the braided diffeomorphism Noether identity for four-dimensional gravity may be expressed similarly to (
6.16
), now with the
4
-form
N_{\mu}^{\star}(F_{e}^{\star},F_{\omega}^{\star},e,\omega)\in\Omega^{4}\big(M,\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{4}\mathbbm{R}^{4})[[\hbar]]
given by

\displaystyle N_{\mu}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\big(\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\mathrm{d}e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(F^{\star}_{e})-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(F^{\star}_{\omega})-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\mathrm{d}F^{\star}_{e})+\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\omega)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\mathrm{d}F^{\star}_{\omega})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\mathrm{d}e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(F^{\star}_{e})-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(F^{\star}_{\omega})-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\mathrm{d}F^{\star}_{e})+\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\omega)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\mathrm{d}F^{\star}_{\omega})\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{1}{4}\,\Big(\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}\mathsf{R}_{l}(\mathrm{d}e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}\big(\mathsf{R}^{l}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+\mathsf{R}^{l}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)+\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}\mathsf{R}_{l}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\curlywedge\mathrm{d}\,\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}\big(\mathsf{R}^{l}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}\mathsf{R}_{l}(e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}\big(\mathsf{R}^{l}(\mathrm{d}e)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+\mathsf{R}^{l}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}e\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\frac{1}{72}\,\big(\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\mathrm{d}e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star})-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(e)\curlywedge\mathrm{d}\,\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\mathrm{d}e)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star})-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(e)\curlywedge\mathrm{d}\,\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star})

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt-\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\curlywedge\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)})+\iota_{\mu}\bar{{\sf f}}^{k}(\omega)\curlywedge\mathrm{d}\,\bar{{\sf f}}_{k}(\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\iota_{\mu}\bar{\sf f}_{k}(\mathrm{d}\omega)\curlywedge\bar{\sf f}^{k}(\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)})+\iota_{\mu}\bar{\sf f}_{k}(\omega)\curlywedge\mathrm{d}\,\bar{\sf f}^{k}(\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)})\big)\ ,

where we defined

\displaystyle\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star}

\displaystyle=e\curlywedge_{\star}[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}+[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e+\mathsf{R}_{l}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{l}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}+6\,\Lambda\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\ ,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star}_{(e,\omega)}

\displaystyle=e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)-\big(\mathsf{R}_{l}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}(e)\big)\curlywedge_{\star}e-\omega\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)+(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\,_{\star\!\!}\triangleleft\omega\ .

We see that the braided Noether identities become extremely complicated in this case, even with the simplified twists that we discussed in the three-dimensional theory, as we see in Example
6.42
below. Nevertheless, the identities exist in explicit form and provide a highly non-trivial differential identification among the braided field equations, which captures the statement of braided gauge invariance of the field theory.

Example 6.42
.

Similarly to Example
6.17
, the expression for the braided diffeomorphism Noether identity can be reduced slightly in the example of the Moyal–Weyl twist
\mathcal{F}_{\theta}
on
M=\mathbbm{R}^{4}
. In this case we can again write the Noether identity as in (
6.18
), now with the
4
-form

\displaystyle N_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big(\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}F^{\star_{\theta}}_{e}-\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}F^{\star_{\theta}}_{\omega}-\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}F^{\star_{\theta}}_{e}+\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}F^{\star_{\theta}}_{\omega}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+F^{\star_{\theta}}_{e}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e-F^{\star_{\theta}}_{\omega}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega-\mathrm{d}F^{\star_{\theta}}_{e}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}e+\mathrm{d}F^{\star_{\theta}}_{\omega}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\omega\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tfrac{1}{4}\,\big(e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega+\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}e+\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}e

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e-\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega-\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e\big)

\displaystyle\quad\,+\tfrac{1}{72}\,\big(\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star_{\theta}}-\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}e\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star_{\theta}}+\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star_{\theta}}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}e-\mathrm{d}\Psi_{(e,\omega)}^{\star_{\theta}}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}e

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt-\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star_{\theta}}_{(e,\omega)}+\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\omega\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star_{\theta}}_{(e,\omega)}-\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star_{\theta}}_{(e,\omega)}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\mathrm{d}\omega+\mathrm{d}\widetilde{\Psi}{}^{\star_{\theta}}_{(e,\omega)}\curlywedge_{\star_{\theta}}\iota_{\mu}^{\star_{\theta}}\omega\big)\ .

Action functional

Twisting the four-dimensional classical ECP pairing from
[
30
, Section 8.2]
using an arbitrary Drinfel’d twist
\mathcal{F}
, we obtain the (braided) cyclic structure

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle(e,\omega)\,,\,(E,{\Omega})\rangle_{\star}&:=\int_{M}\,\mathrm{Tr}\big(e\curlywedge_{\star}E-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}{\Omega}\big)\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\langle(\xi,\rho)\,,\,({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{P}})\rangle_{\star}&:=\int_{M}\,\iota_{\xi}^{\star}{\mathcal{X}}+\int_{M}\,\mathrm{Tr}\big(\rho\curlywedge_{\star}{\mathcal{P}}\big)\ ,\end{split}

(6.43)

on
V_{1}\otimes V_{2}
and
V_{0}\otimes V_{3}
, respectively. We shall write out and simplify the four-dimensional braided noncommutative ECP functional in two steps. First we work with a general twist (or alternatively ignore strict cyclicity for a compatible twist). Then we shall employ a compatible twist, and use
\wedge_{\star}
-cyclicity to simplify the corresponding action functional. The ECP action functional is defined via (
5.39
):

\displaystyle S_{\star}(e,\omega):

\displaystyle\!=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big\langle(e,\omega)\,,\,\ell^{\star}_{1}(e,\omega)\big\rangle_{\star}-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\big\langle(e,\omega)\,,\,\ell^{\star}_{2}\big((e,\omega)\,,\,(e,\omega)\big)\big\rangle_{\star}

\displaystyle\quad\,-\tfrac{1}{24}\,\big\langle(e,\omega)\,,\,\ell^{\star}_{3}\big((e,\omega)\,,\,(e,\omega)\,,\,(e,\omega)\big)\big\rangle_{\star}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\,\frac{1}{6}\,\mathrm{Tr}\big(e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}e+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{24}\,\mathrm{Tr}\Big(e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}+e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt+e\curlywedge_{\star}[\omega,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)

(6.44)

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{l}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{l}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)\curlywedge_{\star}e

\displaystyle\hskip 88.2037pt+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\wedge_{\star}e+6\,\Lambda\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\Big)\ ,

where we used
\ell^{\star}_{1}(e,\omega)=(0,0)
.

We note that the first, second, fifth, sixth, and seventh terms in (
6.3
) combine modulo exact forms as

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{24}\,

\displaystyle\big(2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e+e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}_{k}(e)\curlywedge_{\star}[\mathsf{R}^{k}(\omega),\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}

\displaystyle\,\,+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\tfrac{1}{24}\,\big(2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e-\big(\mathsf{R}_{k}(\omega)\wedge_{\star}\mathsf{R}^{k}(e)\big)\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\omega

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\qquad\qquad+e\curlywedge_{\star}(\omega\wedge_{\star}e)\curlywedge_{\star}\omega-2\,\mathrm{d}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\curlywedge_{\star}\omega+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\tfrac{1}{24}\,\big(2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e-T_{\textrm{\tiny R}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\omega+e\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny L}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}\omega

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\qquad\qquad+e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)\ ,

where in the first equality we used invariance of top-vectors under braided local
\mathfrak{so}(4)
transformations on the third term and integration by parts on the fourth term, while in the second equality we simply identified the left and right torsion 2-forms. The rest of the terms in (
6.3
) may be analogously manipulated into the expression

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{24}\,\big(\omega\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny R}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny L}}^{\star}+\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny R}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\big)\ .

Summing these two expressions, we obtain the
4
-dimensional ECP action functional in the simplified form

\displaystyle S_{\star}(e,\omega)=\frac{1}{24}\,\int_{M}\,\mathrm{Tr}\Big(

\displaystyle 2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e-T_{\textrm{\tiny R}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\omega+e\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny L}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}\omega

(6.45)

\displaystyle\,+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny R}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e-\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny L}}^{\star}+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny R}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)+\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)

\displaystyle\,+e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega+2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e+\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e+6\,\Lambda\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\Big)\ .

The first two terms of (
6.45
) are manifestly braided covariant, while the rest are not individually covariant since the gauge field
\omega
appears explicitly. However, one may check directly that they do form covariant combinations. Let us explicitly demonstrate that the eighth and the last term form a covariant combination. The potential non-covariance arises from the terms proportional to
\mathrm{d}\rho
in a braided
\mathfrak{so}(4)
gauge transformation, since the covariant components proportional to
\rho
vanish by invariance of a top-vector. Hence working modulo exact forms we find

\displaystyle\delta_{\rho}^{\star,{\textrm{\tiny L}}}\big(\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)+\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\rho\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}^{\omega}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)-[\mathrm{d}\rho,\omega]_{\mathfrak{so}(4)}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\rho\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\omega\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\big)-\mathrm{d}\rho\curlywedge_{\star}\big(\omega\triangleright_{\star}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\big)

\displaystyle=0\ ,

where in the second equality we dropped an exact top-form from the first term, and used invariance of top-vectors under the braided
\mathfrak{so}(4)
action on the second term. The rest of the terms in (
6.45
) form covariant combinations via analogous calculations. Hence the invariance of the action functional under braided
\mathfrak{so}(4)
transformations is fulfilled, as it should be by the general discussion of Section
5.8
.

By restricting to compatible twists as in Proposition
4.18
, the pairing becomes a strictly cyclic structure on
\big(V[[\hbar]],\ell_{1}^{\star},\ell_{2}^{\star},\ell_{3}^{\star}\big)
. In this case, the four-dimensional braided ECP action functional simplifies further to

\displaystyle S_{\star}(e,\omega)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\,\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}R^{\star}+\frac{\Lambda}{4}\,e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\Big)

(6.46)

\displaystyle\quad\,-\frac{1}{24}\,\int_{M}\,\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\big(2\,e\curlywedge_{\star}T_{\textrm{\tiny L}}^{\star}-2\,T_{\textrm{\tiny R}}^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e+\mathrm{d}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}^{\omega}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)+\mathrm{d}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny R}}}^{\omega}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\big)\Big)\ \,

which follows immediately from (
6.45
) by
\wedge_{\star}
-cyclicity under the integral and the identity

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\big(\mathrm{d}\omega\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e\big)=\mathrm{Tr}\big(2\,R^{\star}\curlywedge_{\star}e\curlywedge_{\star}e-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny L}}}^{\omega}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\omega\curlywedge_{\star}\mathrm{d}_{\star{\textrm{\tiny R}}}^{\omega}(e\curlywedge_{\star}e)\big)\ ,

that may be checked by a simple expansion of the terms on the right-hand side.
The first line of (
6.46
) is exactly the “naive” deformation of the classical Einstein–Cartan–Palatini action functional in four dimensions, with the correct numerical prefactors; indeed, the second line vanishes in the classical case
{\mathcal{R}}=1\otimes 1
. Using similar identities to those employed before, one can explicitly check that the variation of this action functional with respect to the spin connection
\omega
yields the field equations
\mathcal{F}_{\omega}^{\star}=0
from (
6.3
), while varying with respect to coframe field
e
yields the field equations
\mathcal{F}_{e}^{\star}=0
from (
6.3
), as expected on general grounds from Section
5.8
.
